The Board took the following major decisions in its various meetings 
during the year 2016-17. 
 
(1) The Board in its  meeting held on 20-04-2016 vide  Subject No.-1 
1. Cauvery Water Supply Scheme(CWSS) Stage V- 
Detailed Project  Report for providing 775 MLD of 
Potable water to the 110 villages newly added to BBMP. 
 
------- 
Chief Engineer (K) explained the subject. It was informed that the DPR 
was placed before the Technical Committee meeting on 17/12/2015, 11/03/2016 
and on 04/04/2016 for review and clearance to the DPR. Apart from the various 
suggestions, the TC desired that the provisions for the estimate and the rates 
shall be got checked by Board Engineers before calling of tender.  
M/s. Tata Consulting Services who were entrusted with the preparation of 
DPR for CWSS Stage V made a power point presentation in the Board meeting 
regarding the salient features of the proposed project. The details such as a 
source of water, the various components of the project and the provisions made 
in the estimate was presented. M/s. TCE also explained the main design features 
of the scheme such as the adoption of ‘C’ value of 140, providing 0.5mm thick 
internal epoxy  lining for the transmission line, provision of 8 working pump motor 
sets of 4160 m3 per hour and power requirement of 22 MVA each for the 3 
pumping station.  It was informed that for the additional allocation of 10 TMC of 
Cauvery water, 775 MLD treated water can be supplied.   
After detailed discussion, the Board accorded approval.  
a) To take up CWSS Stage V Scheme to Bangalore City at an 
provisions of the estimate and the rates for various items of work by 
a group of retired Chief Engineers of the Board. 
 
ªÁ¶ðPÀ ªÀgÀ¢ 2016-17


[page 14]
b) 
 
 
 
(2) The Board in its  meeting held on 20-04-2016 vide  Subject No.-5 
5.  Project Consultancy Services for the work of Supply 
of 15MLD tertiary treated water to 2 x 350 MW Gas 
based power plant at Yelahanka from Jakkur sewage 
treatment plant, Bengaluru- regarding approval to 
tender submitted by M/s. TATA Consulting Engineers 
Ltd. for PMC. 
------- 
CE (NI&NW) has explained  subject, placed before the Board seeking 
approval. 
 15 MLD tertiary treated water is required to be supplied to KPCL gas 
bored power plant proposed at Yalahanka and action has been taken to enhance 
the capacity& treating standard of existing Jakkur STP at an estimated amount of 
Rs. 4650 Lakh.  A project monitoring consultant is required to be appointed as 
per the directions in the KTPP act. As such, action was taken to obtain the offers 
from 4 short listed 
and among them, M/s TATA Consulting Engineers Ltd. has quoted the lowest 
offer of  Rs 119.00 Lakh. 
After detailed deliberation, Board accorded approval to appoint M/s TATA 
Consulting Engineers Ltd as a Project Monitoring Consultant to the work of 
Supply of 15MLD tertiary treated water to 2 x 350 MW Gas based power plant at 
Yelahanka from Jakkur sewage treatment plant, Bengaluru at the offered price of 
Rs. 1,19,00,000-00 excluding Service tax and Service tax to be paid as 
applicable. 
(3) The Board in its meeting held on 20-04-2016 vide Subject No.-11

(6) The Board in its  meeting held on 2-07-2017 vide  Subject No.-7 
7.  BWSSP-II: Contract S1b: Sewage Treatment Plant at 
Kengeri (Turnkey Contract) under JICA Loan-ID-P168-
Construction of additional Civil structures for ultimate 
build out Capacity of 80MLD – Approval for Variation-II. 
------- 
       EIC explained the subject of the work of construction of 60MLD Sewage 
Treatment Plant at Kengeri (Turnkey Contract). 
 
ªÁ¶ðPÀ ªÀgÀ¢ 2016-17


[page 17]
           EIC explained the reasons for Variation-II, and it would be advantageous 
for Construction of additional civil structures for ultimate design capacity now 
itself, because of the following facts, 
1. The size of some of the units like Anmoxzone , etc., can be optimized if 
the design and execution is considered for ultimate build out capacity of 
80 MLD at the beginning itself. 
 
2. Kengeri STP has anaerobic digesters, which produce bio gas which is 
rich in Methane. Therefore some of the units like digesters and gas 
holders are considered as explosive zones and if the execution has to 
be carried out in the future, the link up of pipe lines & building is 
extremely sensitive which can be avoided by taking up the expansion 
now itself. 
3. Optimum utilization of common units such as Distribution chambers, 
TSPS, Bioreactors, Anamox Zone, Power supply system of STP can be 
achieved 
        It was also explained that this Variation-II has to be taken up under FIDIC 
conditions Clauses 13.1 (e) and proposal will be submitted to JICA after the 
approval of the Board. After concurrence of JICA for these variations even if the 
Board has to meet the additional expenditure from its own funds the proposal, 
will be covered under the project aided by JICA. Hence, KTPP Act will not be 
applicable to under clause (3). However, if the JICA does not agree to take it as 
variation the same work  would have to be under taken by the Board on its own 
and the exemption will be sought from the Government under Clause 4(C) of 
KTPP Act.  
 
      The Board after detailed discussion, accorded approval for the Variation-II 
amounting to Rs. 22.20Crores as stated above.

16.  The work of Supply of 15 MLD tertiary treated water to 2 
X 350 MW Gas based power plant at Yelahanka from 
Jakkur  sewage treatment plant, Bengaluru- regarding 
approval to entrust the tender. 
------- 
 
CE (P) explained the subject of the reuse project taken up under Deposit 
Contribution works for KPC Ltd, wherein BWSSB has agreed to supply required 
15 MLD treated water to proposed power plant.  This subject was got approved 
in the Board meeting held on 26.03.2016 for Rs. 62.08 Crore towards the capital 
work and Rs. 15.45 Crore operation and maintenance for 7 years. 
Work involves increasing the treatment capacity of the existing 10 MLD 
secondary sewerag
PMC consultants and Tender Scrutiny Committee (Technical and Finance), it is 
found M/s Passavant Energy & Environment India Pvt Ltd is the lowest among 
-00.  
After discussions, the Board cleared the subject to entrust the above work 
to M/s Passavant Energy & Environment India Pvt Ltd at a cost of Rs. 
58,86,50,000-00 (12% above the estimate) after entering into necessary 
agreement as per tender conditions.

(9) The Board in its  meeting held on 2-07-2016 vide  Subject No.-21 
21.  Estimate for the work of providing Water supply 
facilities to villages surrounding Sir.  M. 
Vishweswarayya BDA Layout under Ward No. 72- 
Herohalli of BBMP. 
------- 
CE(P) explained the subject and informed that BDA have requested to provide 
forming Sir M. Vishveswaraiah layout. Accordingly, estimates total amounting to 
Rs.45.48 Crore were forwarded to BDA for counter signature and deposition of funds 
during 2012-13.  BDA have deposited an initial amount of Rs.15.00 Crore and 
requested to take up the works.  After detailed discussion, Board accorded 
administrative approval for: 
1. Estimate for the work of providing Water supply facilities to Vigneshwar 
Nagar & Muneshwar Nagar  amounting to Rs. 10,61,00,000/-, 
2. Estimate for the work of providing Water supply facilities to Tunga 
Nagara, Prasanna Badavane, Maruthi Nagara, KSRTC Badavane & 
Javaregowda Badavane amounting to Rs. 23,84,00,000/-, 
3. Estimate for the work of providing Water supply facilities to Sonnenahalli, 
Ullalu, Ullalu Upanagara, Gidadakonenahalli, Karekallu, Bilekallu, 
Muddinapalya, Ullalu Basthi, Chikkabasthi (Balance portion) and 
Doddabasthi (Balance Portion) amounting to Rs. 11,03,00,000/-, 
4. To Call for Short term Tenders 
5. BDA shall be requested to deposit the balance amount.  The work  
    orders can only be issued after receiving the whole amount from the  
    BDA.

28.  Providing Water Supply & Sewerage System to 110 
Villages included to BBMP- ..

CE (P) explained the subject  in detail and after detailed discussion, Board

Karnataka and 7 ½% from the Board, for obtaining Administrative 
approval.

funds/AMRUT/Government of Karnataka/BWSSB funds.

(19) The Board in its  meeting held on 27-10-2016 vide  Subject No.-30 
30.    Work of Providing Water Supply facilities coming 
under 110 villages of BBMP.  
------- 
CE (P) explained the subject in detail. After detailed discussion, Board to 
accord approval for the following. 
1. To invite tenders for the Work of Providing Water Supply facilities 
com
E-Procurement adopting KW-4 document of KTPP act giving 45 Days 
for tender period. 
2. To include following Clause 3.4 in the IFT: 
ªÁ¶ðPÀ ªÀgÀ¢ 2016-17


[page 27]
“To qualify for a package of contracts made up of this and other 
contracts for which tenders are invited in this IFT, the Tenderer must 
demonstrate having experience and resources to meet the aggregate of 
the qualifying criteria for the individual contracts.” 
3. To call for a short term tender for appointing Project Management 
Consultancy services for the work of Providing Water Supply facilities 
coming under 110 villages of BBMP.

29) The Board in its  meeting held on 31-03-2017 vide  Subject No.-18 
18. Amendment to the existing contracts-Jurisdictions 
of the courts and safety measures to be taken while 
executing the works-reg.  
------- 
Chief Engineer (P) explained the subject in detail regarding new proposal 
to amend the contractual conditions (both for the existing and in future) through 
an amendment to the agreement duly highlighting the necessity of providing 
safety gadgets to the working personnel’s and taking necessary precautions 
failing which in the event of any untoward incidents, the contractor is made 
explicitly liable to pay suitable compensation to the families of the deceased. 
The Board directed to incorporate the following additional points: 
1. In case of any death or accident the contract will be cancelled after 
following due process. 
2. The Board will be entitled to claim exemplary damages from the 
contractor in case of death of any work man/ employees of the 
contractor in any of the BWSSB works as it damages the reputation 
of the Board. 
After detailed discussions, Board has accorded approval to Amendment to 
the existing Contract and also for future contract regarding Jurisdictions of the 
courts and Safety Measures.

PREFACE
The Bangalore Water Supply & Sewerage Board was established in the year 
1964. The Board has envisaged and implemented many innovative schemes 
to supply adequate amount of water to the citizens of Bangalore. When it was 
found that Thippegondanahalli Reservoir was inadequate to meet the city's 
water needs, BWSSB conceived the Cauvery Water Supply Scheme and 
brought Cauvery Water to Bangalore in the first Phase of the scheme in the 
year 1974 and this was followed by successful implementation of the 
remaining three phases of the scheme. BWSSB is currently supplying 500 
MLD of water form all the four stages of the Cauvery Water Supply Scheme to 
the city of Bangalore. BWSSB has successfully completed the 2nd Phase of 
the 4th Stage of Cauvery Water Supply Scheme to bring additional 440 MLD of 
Water to Bangalore City.
The challenges faced by BWSSB has increased with the addition of civic local 
bodies around the city. The Board is facing an inevitable task of quenching 
the thirst of around one crore people in the city for the purpose adoption of 
water harvesting which is a water conservation tool needs to the made 
mandatory. Besides water supply, BWSSB is also entrusted with the 
enormous responsibiity of treating the waste water.
The Annual report of 2017-18, which is prepared in accordance with the 
guidelines stipuated in Section 86(2) of Bangalore Water Supply and 
Sewerage Board Act 1964, reflects the financial activities and the 
performance of the board. I would like to thank Engineer - in - Chief, all the 
Chief Engineers, Chief Administrative officer, Financial Advisor and other 
Officials who have extended their co-operation for the preparation of this 
report.
CHAIRMAN
BWSSB

[page 61]
ANNUAL REPORT FOR THE YEAR 2016-17 
 
INTRODUCTION 
 
(I) Main Objection & Functions 
 
Bangalore Water Supply & Sewerage Board (BWSSB) is 
responsible for providing water supply, sewerage system and 
sewage disposal in the Bangalore Metropolitan Area. BWSSB has 
implemented and commissioned the Cauvery Water Supply 
Scheme Stages I, II, III, IV Phase II with a designed potential of 
1340 MLD of treated water.  
 
a. Providing water supply and making adequate provision for 
sewerage and disposal of the waste water in existing and 
developing areas of Bangalore Metropolitan area. 
 
b. Preparation and implementation of plans and water supply 
schemes to meet the increasing demand. 
 
c. Preparation and implementation of plans and schemes for 
providing proper water supply sewerage and disposal of waste 
water of the city. 
 
d. Remolding the distribution system to supply the available water 
equitably. 
 
e.  launching new schemes. 
 
f. Improvement to the existing sewerage system. 
 
ªÁ¶ðPÀ ªÀgÀ¢ 2016-17


[page 62]
g. Revenue collections for the water supply sewerage and waste 
water disposal system. 
ii)  Bangalore continues to be one of the fastest growing cities in 
India, with decadal growth of 50.70%. According to 2011 census, 
the population of Bangalore city has reached 96 Lakhs in the 
Bangalore Metropolitan area and crossed one crore by 2017. 
Presently the total quantity for water available from all sources 
under normal conditions is 1740 MLD against which 914.00 MLD 
was received during 2016-17. The per capital water supply at 
present is about 100 to 110 liters per capita per day (LPCD) after 
deducting supplies to major industries and losses in the 
distribution system. This is far below the national standard 
recommended by CPHEEO, which is 135 LPCD for a 105 LPCD 
will dwindle down to 90 LPCD by 2017 unless further 
augmentation schemes are taken up. The Bangalore Metropolitan 
area (BMA) has also considerably increased, and as per present 
BMA the area comprises 800 sq.kms.Thus there is an urgent need 
for augmenting the existing water supply system of Bangalore City. 
 
ªÁ¶ðPÀ ªÀgÀ¢ 2016-17


[page 63]
Chairman and Members 
 
is assisted by One Engineer-in-Chief four Chief Engineers, one chief 
Administrative Officer cum Secretary, One Financial Advisor and Chief
Account Officer (Revenue/Expenditure/Projects), a Public Relations
Officer and other Officers to assist him in the matter of day-today
administration. Organization chart is appended in Annexure-II.
Organizational Structure 
 
The Board was formed by an Act of State Legislature vide 
.1964. 
The management of the Board is vested in a Board of 9 members 
including the Chairman, who are appointed by the Government. 
During the year under report Sri T.M. Vijaya Bhaskar, I.A.S and 
Sri Tushar Giri Nath, I.A.S were the Chairperson of the Board.

The Board took the following major decisions in its various meetings 
during the year 2016-17 
(1) The Board in its  meeting held on 20-04-2016 vide  Subject No.-1 
1. Cauvery Water Supply Scheme(CWSS) Stage V- 
Detailed Project  Report for providing 775 MLD of 
Potable water to the 110 villages newly added to BBMP. 
 
------- 
Chief Engineer (K) explained the subject. It was informed that the DPR 
was placed before the Technical Committee meeting on 17/12/2015, 11/03/2016 
and on 04/04/2016 for review and clearance to the DPR. Apart from the various 
suggestions, the TC desired that the provisions for the estimate and the rates 
shall be got checked by Board Engineers before calling of tender.  
M/s. Tata Consulting Services who were entrusted with the preparation of 
DPR for CWSS Stage V made a power point presentation in the Board meeting 
regarding the salient features of the proposed project. The details such as a 
source of water, the various components of the project and the provisions made 
in the estimate was presented. M/s. TCE also explained the main design features 
of the scheme such as the adoption of ‘C’ value of 140, providing 0.5mm thick 
internal epoxy  lining for the transmission line, provision of 8 working pump motor 
sets of 4160 m3 per hour and power requirement of 22 MVA each for the 3 
pumping station.  It was informed that for the additional allocation of 10 TMC of 
Cauvery water, 775 MLD treated water can be supplied.   
After detailed discussion, the Board accorded approval.  
c) To take up CWSS Stage V Scheme to Bangalore City at an 
provisions of the estimate and the rates for various items of work by 
a group of retired Chief Engineers of the Board.

(6) The Board in its  meeting held on 2-07-2017 vide  Subject No.-7 
7.  BWSSP-II: Contract S1b:Sewage Treatment Plant at 
Kengeri (Turnkey Contract) under JICA Loan-ID-P168-
Construction of additional Civil structures for ultimate 
build out Capacity of 80MLD – Approval for Variation-II. 
------- 
ªÁ¶ðPÀ ªÀgÀ¢ 2016-17


[page 73]
       EIC explained the subject of the work of construction of 60MLD Sewage 
Treatment Plant at Kengeri (Turnkey Contract). 
 
           EIC explained the reasons for Variation-II, and it would be advantageous 
for Construction of additional civil structures for ultimate design capacity now 
itself, because of the following facts, 
4. The size of some of the units like Anmoxzone , etc., can be optimized if 
the design and execution is considered for ultimate build out capacity of 
80 MLD at the beginning itself. 
 
5. Kengeri STP has anaerobic digesters, which produce bio gas which is 
rich in Methane. Therefore some of the units like digesters and gas 
holders are considered as explosive zones and if the execution has to 
be carried out in the future, the link up of pipe lines & building is 
extremely sensitive which can be avoided by taking up the expansion 
now itself. 
6. Optimum utilization of common units such as Distribution chambers, 
TSPS, Bioreactors, Anamox Zone, Power supply system of STP can be 
achieved 
        It was also explained that this Variation-II has to be taken up under FIDIC 
conditions Clauses 13.1 (e) and proposal will be submitted to JICA after the 
approval of the Board. After concurrence of JICA for these variations even if the 
Board has to meet the additional expenditure from its own funds the proposal, 
will be covered under the project aided by JICA. Hence, KTPP Act will not be 
applicable to under clause (3). However, if the JICA does not agree to take it as 
variation the same work  would have to be under taken by the Board on its own 
and the exemption will be sought from the Government under Clause 4(C) of 
KTPP Act.  
 
      The Board after detailed discussion, accorded approval for the Variation-II 
amounting to Rs. 22.20Crores as stated above.  
ªÁ¶ðPÀ ªÀgÀ¢ 2016-17


[page 74]

21.  Estimate for the work of providing Water supply 
facilities to villages surrounding Sir.  M. 
Vishweswarayya BDA Layout under Ward No. 72- 
Herohalli of BBMP. 
------- 
CE(P) explained the subject and informed that BDA have requested to provide 
water supply a
forming Sir M. Vishveswaraiah layout. Accordingly, estimates total amounting to 
Rs.45.48 Crore were forwarded to BDA for counter signature and deposition of funds 
during 2012-13.  BDA have deposited an initial amount of Rs.15.00 Crore and 
requested to take up the works.  After detailed discussion, Board accorded 
administrative approval for: 
1. Estimate for the work of providing Water supply facilities to Vigneshwar 
Nagar & Muneshwar Nagar  amounting to Rs. 10,61,00,000/-, 
2. Estimate for the work of providing Water supply facilities to Tunga

(19) The Board in its  meeting held on 27-10-2016 vide  Subject No.-30 
30.    Work of Providing Water Supply facilities coming 
under 110 villages of BBMP.  
------- 
CE(P) explained the subject in detail. After detailed discussion, Board to 
accord approval for the following. 
1. To invite tenders for the Work of Providing Water Supply facilities 
E-Procurement adopting KW-4 document of KTPP act giving 45 Days 
for tender period. 
2. To include following Clause 3.4 in the IFT: 
“To qualify for a package of contracts made up of this and other 
contracts for which tenders are invited in this IFT, the Tenderer must 
demonstrate having experience and resources to meet the aggregate of 
the qualifying criteria for the individual contracts.” 
3. To call for a short term tender for appointing Project Management 
Consultancy services for the work of Providing Water Supply facilities 
coming under 110 villages of BBMP.

28) The Board in its  meeting held on 31-03-2017 vide  Subject No.-18 
18. Amendment to the existing contracts-Jurisdictions 
of the courts and safety measures to be taken while 
executing the works-reg.  
------- 
Chief Engineer (P) explained the subject in detail regarding new proposal 
to amend the contractual conditions (both for the existing and in future) through 
an amendment to the agreement duly highlighting the necessity of providing 
safety gadgets to the working personnel’s and taking necessary precautions 
ªÁ¶ðPÀ ªÀgÀ¢ 2016-17


[page 91]
failing which in the event of any untoward incidents, the contractor is made 
explicitly liable to pay suitable compensation to the families of the deceased. 
The Board directed to incorporate the following additional points: 
3.  In case of any death or accident the contract will be cancelled after 
following due process. 
4.  The Board will be entitled to claim exemplary damages from the 
contractor in case of death of any work man/ employees of the 
contractor in any of the BWSSB works as it damages the reputation 
of the Board. 
After detailed discussions, Board has accorded approval to Amendment to 
the existing Contract and also for future contract regarding Jurisdictions of the 
courts and Safety Measures.

Chief Engineer (Kaveri) 
Annual Report and major projects of 2016-17 taken up under CE(K) 
Zone 
 
 Cauvery Water Supply Scheme Stage-IV Phase-II 
 
The Cauvery Water Supply Scheme Stage-IV phase-II is taken up at 
JICA. An additional 500 MLD of water is augmented and commissioned 
on 31.10.2012. At present around 440 MLD of water is supplied to the 
City. Under this Scheme Water Supply & Sanitation facilities are 
provided to 164 slums of the City.  
 
 The Government of Karnataka has given Administrative approval to 
the revised estimated cost of Rs. 5773 crores vide GO No: UDD 120 
MNI 2010, dated: 30.12.2015.  
 
 Under the Sewerage component 10 Sewage Treatment Plants (STP) 
are constructed to treat 336 MLD of Sewage. The Progress Details of 
the STP’s are as shown below:-

 Other Works:- 
 
1. The construction of  300 MLD Water Treatment Plant at T.K. Halli in 
lieu of the Water Treatment Plants constructed under Stage-I & II is taken 
up at an estimated cost of Rs. 148 crores. The funding pattern for this work 
is 35% by JnNURM, 15% by GOK & loan from KUIDFC (Rs. 52 crores). 
 
The above work has been awarded to M/s. Degremont, France & 
Degremont Pvt. Ltd., (JV) at a cost of Rs. 148.01 crores. The work is under 
38.20% is achieved. 
 
2. The work of Extension of Additional raw water gravity pipeline from 
Netkal Balancing Reservoir to T.K. Halli is taken up at an estimated cost of 
Rs. 160.33 crores. The funding pattern is KUIDFC (Rs. 80 crores), GOK 
(Rs. 40 crores) & BWSSB (Rs. 40.33 cores). 
 
The above work is awarded to M/s. Sai Sudhir Infrastructure at a cost 
of Rs. 167.60 crores. The date of commencement is 09.02.2017 & date of 
completion is 08.02.2019.  At present Procurement of Pipes is under 
progress.

Chief Engineer (Maintenance) 
ANNUAL REPORT FOR 2016-17  
MAINTENANCE ZONE 
 
Maintenance zone headed by Engineer-in-Chief supported by one Chief 
Engineer, four Additional Chief Engineers and Two Deputy Chief Engineers, Three 
Technical Assistants along with eleven Executive Engineers and 31 Assistant Executive 
Engineers. The maintenance zone is performing following duties; 
1. Operation and maintenance of water supply distribution system maintenance of 
sewer laterals & sewerage system upto 400mm dia pipeline. 
2. Sanction of water supply & sanitary house service connections by collecting 
meter deposits, prorata charges etc., 
3. Generation & issue of monthly water bills, collection of water revenue, 
disconnection of arrears accumulated connections & replacement of defective 
meters etc.,  
4. Improvements of water supply and sewerage system by replacement, 
rehabilitation and upgradation by providing higher dia water supply and UGD 
pipelines in the required areas. 
5. Execution of water supply and UGD pipeline works under Board Funds and also 
under DC Works of BBMP, BDA, BMRCL and Private Parties.  
6. Attending public complaints with respect of water supply, water supply pipeline 
attending and solving water bill disputes. 
7. Attending all RTI cases, HRC cases, LA/LC questions, Court cases and 
Government correspondence. 
8. Issue of permissions for drilling of new Borewells after Geo Physical 
investigation. 
ªÁ¶ðPÀ ªÀgÀ¢ 2016-17


[page 97]
9. Conducting DCB/monthly Progress review meeting, Appeal Committee Meeting 
& Water Adalath etc.,

March-2017 is 9.28 lakhs.

16.  Revenue billing and Collection system (SAJALA) w.r.t 
2016-17 
 
a. Centralized Billing and Collection system (SAJALA) is citizen centric 
sustainable manner. 
b. As per the distribution network the Centralized billing system provides very 
important feed back as far as water demand managements/revenue losses 
are concerned and helps in decision making. 
c. Quick MIS for on the spot analysis of important parameters like online 
Category wise Consumption pattern analysis, list of defaulters and total 
working and Non-working meters and Demand collection Balance statements 
etc., can be taken out area wise / ward wise / Constituency wise / sub division 
wise / Division wise for better decision making. 
d. 
improve Collect  
e. Total KIOSKs for Revenue Collection : 74 Nos. 
f. Online Payment can be made through 5 gateways. 
g. Six months bill details can be viewed through online. 
h. SMS sent to consumers reminding regarding payment to be made. 
i. ECS option is available for collection of payment. 
j. Payment can be made through Bangalore One centers. 
k. Payment can be made through Easy Bill. 
l. Payment can be made through Karnataka mobile apps.
